Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Takahashi is solving quizzes. He has easily solved all but the last one.
The last quiz has three choices: 1, 2, and 3.
With his supernatural power, Takahashi has found out that the choices A and B are both wrong.
Print the correct choice for this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - Each of the numbers A and B is 1, 2, or 3.
 - A and B are different.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
A
B

-----Output-----
Print the correct choice.

-----Sample Input-----
3
1

-----Sample Output-----
2

When we know 3 and 1 are both wrong, the correct choice is 2.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are 3 possible choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. We know that two of these choices (A and B) are wrong
3. We need to find the remaining correct choice
4. A and B are different values (so they're not the same)

## Solution Approach

Since there are only 3 choices (1, 2, 3) and we know two of them are wrong (A and B), the correct answer must be the one that's left.

We can solve this by:
1. Reading the two wrong choices (A and B)
2. Finding which number from {1, 2, 3} is not equal to either A or B

## Step-by-step solution

1. Read A and B from input
2. The correct answer is the number that is not A and not B
3. Since the choices are only 1, 2, 3, we can check which one is missing

## Implementation

```python
# Read the two wrong choices
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct choice is the one that's not A and not B
# Since we have choices 1, 2, 3 and two are wrong, the remaining one is correct
choices = [1, 2, 3]
correct_choice = [choice for choice in choices if choice != A and choice != B][0]

print(correct_choice)
```

## Verification with sample:
- Input: A=3, B=1
- Choices: [1, 2, 3]
- Check each choice:
  - 1: 1 == A? No, 1 == B? Yes → wrong
  - 2: 2 == A? No, 2 == B? No → correct
  - 3: 3 == A? Yes → wrong
- So correct choice is 2 ✓

The solution works correctly.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are three choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. We know that two of the choices (A and B) are wrong
3. We need to find the remaining correct choice
4. A and B are different values (so they can't be the same)

## Logic

Since there are only three choices (1, 2, 3) and we know two of them are wrong (A and B), the correct answer must be the one that's not A and not B.

We can solve this by:
- Reading the two wrong choices A and B
- Finding which number from {1, 2, 3} is missing when we exclude A and B

## Step-by-step solution

1. Read the values of A and B from input
2. The correct choice is the one that's not A and not B
3. Since we have three options (1, 2, 3), we can simply check which one is not equal to A or B

## Implementation approach

The simplest approach is to check which of the three numbers (1, 2, 3) is not equal to A or B.

```python
# Read input
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct choice is the one that's not A and not B
# Since we have choices 1, 2, 3 and two are wrong (A and B),
# the correct one must be the remaining one.

# We can find it by checking which number from [1,2,3] is not A or B
choices = [1, 2, 3]
for choice in choices:
    if choice != A and choice != B:
        print(choice)
        break
```

Let me verify with the sample:
- A = 3, B = 1
- Check choice 1: 1 == 1, so not correct
- Check choice 2: 2 != 3 and 2 != 1, so correct
- Output: 2 ✓

This matches the expected output.

```python
# Read input
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# Find the correct choice (the one not equal to A or B)
choices = [1, 2, 3]
for choice in choices:
    if choice != A and choice != B:
        print(choice)
        break
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are three choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. We know two of the choices (A and B) are wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (the one that's not A or B)
4. A and B are different values (so we don't have duplicates)

## Solution Approach

Since there are only three possible choices (1, 2, 3) and we know two of them are wrong, the correct answer must be the one that's left.

The strategy is simple:
- Read the two wrong choices (A and B)
- The correct choice is the one from {1, 2, 3} that is not equal to A or B

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read A and B from input
2. Create a set of all possible choices {1, 2, 3}
3. Remove A and B from this set
4. The remaining element is the answer

Alternatively, we can use a simpler approach: since we know the choices are 1, 2, 3 and only one is correct, we can check which number from 1, 2, 3 is not equal to A or B.

## Implementation

```python
# Read the two wrong choices
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct choice is the one that's not A or B
# Since the choices are 1, 2, 3, we can check each one
for choice in [1, 2, 3]:
    if choice != A and choice != B:
        print(choice)
        break
```

## Verification with Sample

- Input: A=3, B=1
- Check choice 1: 1 ≠ 3? Yes, but 1 ≠ 1? No → not correct
- Check choice 2: 2 ≠ 3? Yes, 2 ≠ 1? Yes → correct
- Output: 2 ✓

This matches the expected output.

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are 3 choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. We know that two of the choices (A and B) are wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (the one that's not A or B)
4. A and B are different values (so they can't be the same)

## Solution Steps

1. Read the two wrong choices A and B
2. Since there are only 3 choices (1, 2, 3), and we know two of them are wrong, the correct choice must be the one that's missing
3. We can find the correct choice by checking which number from {1, 2, 3} is not equal to A or B

## Algorithm

The correct choice is simply the number that is not A and not B. Since the set of choices is {1, 2, 3}, we can find the missing one by:
- If A = 1, B = 2, then correct = 3
- If A = 1, B = 3, then correct = 2  
- If A = 2, B = 3, then correct = 1

Alternatively, we can think of it as: correct = 1 + 2 + 3 - A - B = 6 - A - B

## Implementation

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct choice is the one that's not A and not B
# Since the choices are 1, 2, 3, the sum of all choices is 6
# So the correct choice is 6 - A - B
correct_choice = 6 - A - B

print(correct_choice)
```

## Verification with Sample
- Input: A = 3, B = 1
- correct_choice = 6 - 3 - 1 = 2
- Output: 2 ✓

This matches the expected sample output.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are three choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. Takahashi knows that choices A and B are both wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (which must be the remaining one)

## Logic Analysis

Since there are only three choices (1, 2, 3) and two of them (A and B) are wrong, the correct choice must be the one that is not A and not B.

So the solution is: correct_choice = 1, 2, or 3 (the one that's different from A and B).

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read the two wrong choices A and B
2. Since there are only three choices (1, 2, 3), the correct answer is the one that's not A and not B
3. We can find this by checking which number from {1, 2, 3} is missing when we remove A and B

## Implementation Approach

The simplest approach is to create a set of all possible choices {1, 2, 3} and remove A and B, then find what remains.

Alternatively, we can use the mathematical fact that 1 + 2 + 3 = 6, so the correct answer is 6 - A - B.

## Code Implementation

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The sum of all choices (1+2+3) is 6
# If we remove A and B, the remaining one is 6 - A - B
correct_choice = 6 - A - B

print(correct_choice)
```

## Verification with Sample

- Sample input: A=3, B=1
- Correct choice = 6 - 3 - 1 = 2
- This matches the expected output

The solution works because:
- A + B + correct = 6 (since 1+2+3=6)
- Therefore, correct = 6 - A - B
- Since A and B are different and both from {1,2,3}, the result will always be one of {1,2,3}

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are 3 possible choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. Two of the choices (A and B) are wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (the one that's not A or B)
4. A and B are guaranteed to be different

## Solution Approach

Since there are only 3 choices (1, 2, 3) and we know two of them are wrong (A and B), the correct answer must be the remaining one.

We can solve this by:
1. Read the two wrong choices A and B
2. Find which of the three numbers (1, 2, 3) is not equal to A or B
3. Output that number

## Step-by-step Logic

1. The three possible choices are 1, 2, 3
2. If A and B are both wrong, then the correct answer is the one that doesn't appear in {A, B}
3. We can use the fact that the sum of 1+2+3 = 6
4. If we remove A and B from the sum, what's left is the correct choice
5. So: correct_choice = 6 - A - B

Alternatively, we can use a simple conditional check, but the mathematical approach is more elegant.

## Verification with Sample

- Input: A=3, B=1
- Correct choice = 6 - 3 - 1 = 2 ✓
- This matches the expected output

## Implementation

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The sum of all choices (1+2+3) minus the two wrong ones gives us the correct one
correct_choice = 6 - A - B

print(correct_choice)
```

The solution works because:
- 1 + 2 + 3 = 6
- A and B are two wrong choices (so they sum to some value)
- The remaining number (the correct choice) must be 6 - A - B
- Since A and B are different and both from {1,2,3}, this will always give exactly one of {1,2,3} as the answer

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. There are 3 choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. We know that choices A and B are both wrong
3. We need to find the correct choice (which must be the remaining one)

## Step-by-step Solution

### Step 1: Analyze the problem
- Since there are only 3 choices (1, 2, 3) and we know 2 of them are wrong, the correct answer must be the one that is left out.
- The correct choice is the one that is not equal to A or B.

### Step 2: Identify the pattern
- If we have three numbers: 1, 2, 3
- If A and B are two of them, then the correct answer is the third one.
- We can find this by checking which number from {1, 2, 3} is not equal to A or B.

### Step 3: Algorithm
1. Read the input values A and B
2. The correct answer is the number from {1, 2, 3} that is not equal to A or B
3. We can find this by checking each possibility:
   - If A ≠ 1 and B ≠ 1, then answer is 1
   - If A ≠ 2 and B ≠ 2, then answer is 2  
   - If A ≠ 3 and B ≠ 3, then answer is 3

Alternatively, we can use a simpler approach:
- The sum of all choices is 1 + 2 + 3 = 6
- The correct answer = 6 - A - B

### Step 4: Verify with sample
- Sample input: A = 3, B = 1
- Correct answer = 6 - 3 - 1 = 2 ✓
- This matches the expected output.

### Step 5: Implementation

```python
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The sum of all choices is 1 + 2 + 3 = 6
# Since A and B are wrong, the correct choice is 6 - A - B
correct_choice = 6 - A - B

print(correct_choice)
```

The solution works because:
- The total sum of all three choices is 6 (1 + 2 + 3)
- Since A and B are both wrong, they are not the correct answer
- Therefore, the correct answer must be 6 - A - B
- This gives us exactly the remaining choice that is not A or B

## Problem Understanding

1. There are 3 choices: 1, 2, and 3
2. We know two of the choices (A and B) are wrong
3. We need to find the remaining correct choice
4. A and B are different values (so we won't have duplicates)

## Logic

Since there are only 3 choices (1, 2, 3) and we know two are wrong (A and B), the correct answer must be the one that is not A and not B.

We can solve this by:
1. Reading the two wrong choices A and B
2. Finding which number from {1, 2, 3} is missing from {A, B}

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read A from input
2. Read B from input
3. The correct answer is the number that is not A and not B
4. We can check which of 1, 2, 3 is different from both A and B

## Implementation

```python
# Read inputs
A = int(input())
B = int(input())

# The correct choice is the one that is not A and not B
# Since there are only 3 choices (1, 2, 3), we can check each one
# The correct answer must be the one that doesn't equal A or B

        print(choice)
        break
```

- Check choice 1: 1 == 3? No, 1 == 1? Yes → not valid
- Check choice 2: 2 == 3? No, 2 == 1? No → valid!

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

And while Mishka is enjoying her trip...

Chris is a little brown bear. No one knows, where and when he met Mishka, but for a long time they are together (excluding her current trip). However, best friends are important too. John is Chris' best friend.

Once walking with his friend, John gave Chris the following problem:

At the infinite horizontal road of width w, bounded by lines y = 0 and y = w, there is a bus moving, presented as a convex polygon of n vertices. The bus moves continuously with a constant speed of v in a straight Ox line in direction of decreasing x coordinates, thus in time only x coordinates of its points are changing. Formally, after time t each of x coordinates of its points will be decreased by vt.

There is a pedestrian in the point (0, 0), who can move only by a vertical pedestrian crossing, presented as a segment connecting points (0, 0) and (0, w) with any speed not exceeding u. Thus the pedestrian can move only in a straight line Oy in any direction with any speed not exceeding u and not leaving the road borders. The pedestrian can instantly change his speed, thus, for example, he can stop instantly.

Please look at the sample note picture for better understanding.

We consider the pedestrian is hit by the bus, if at any moment the point he is located in lies strictly inside the bus polygon (this means that if the point lies on the polygon vertex or on its edge, the pedestrian is not hit by the bus).

You are given the bus position at the moment 0. Please help Chris determine minimum amount of time the pedestrian needs to cross the road and reach the point (0, w) and not to be hit by the bus.

Input

The first line of the input contains four integers n, w, v, u (3 ≤ n ≤ 10 000, 1 ≤ w ≤ 109, 1 ≤ v, u ≤ 1000) — the number of the bus polygon vertices, road width, bus speed and pedestrian speed respectively.

The next n lines describes polygon vertices in counter-clockwise order. i-th of them contains pair of integers xi and yi ( - 109 ≤ xi ≤ 109, 0 ≤ yi ≤ w) — coordinates of i-th polygon point. It is guaranteed that the polygon is non-degenerate.

Output

Print the single real t — the time the pedestrian needs to croos the road and not to be hit by the bus. The answer is considered correct if its relative or absolute error doesn't exceed 10 - 6.

Example

Input

5 5 1 2
1 2
3 1
4 3
3 4
1 4


Output

5.0000000000

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a bus moving leftward (decreasing x coordinates) with speed `v`
2. A pedestrian starts at (0,0) and needs to reach (0,w) moving vertically
3. The pedestrian can move with maximum speed `u`
4. We need to find the minimum time for the pedestrian to cross without being hit by the bus
5. Being hit means being strictly inside the bus polygon (not on edges or vertices)

## Key Insights

1. The pedestrian needs to cross the road from (0,0) to (0,w)
2. The time to cross is `w/u` if they could go straight through without constraints
3. However, the bus is moving leftward, so if the pedestrian is at a certain height at time `t`, the bus will have moved left by `v*t` at that time
4. We need to find the minimum time such that at no point during the journey is the pedestrian strictly inside the bus

## Strategy

The pedestrian can optimize their path by moving at different speeds at different heights. The key insight is that we need to find when the pedestrian should reach each height to avoid being hit.

For a given time `t`, the pedestrian will have reached height `h = u * t'` where `t'` is the time spent at that height. But more importantly, at time `t` when the pedestrian reaches height `h`, the bus has moved left by `v*t`.

We need to check if at any moment during the crossing, the pedestrian is strictly inside the bus polygon.

## Geometric Approach

For each height `y` in [0, w], we need to find if there exists a time `t` such that:
- The pedestrian is at height `y`
- At that time, the bus has moved left by `v*t`
- The pedestrian's position (x=0, y) is strictly inside the bus polygon at that time

Since the pedestrian moves vertically, and we want to minimize time, we can use binary search on the answer.

For a given time `T`, we check if the pedestrian can reach (0,w) without being hit:
- At time `t` (when pedestrian reaches height `y`), the pedestrian is at (0, y)
- The bus has moved left by `v*t`
- We need to check if (0, y) is strictly inside the polygon shifted left by `v*t`

## Algorithm

1. The pedestrian can go from (0,0) to (0,w) with total time at least `w/u`
2. We need to check if at any point during this journey, the pedestrian is inside the bus
3. For a fixed time `T`, we can compute the position of the pedestrian at each height
4. We use binary search on `T` to find the minimum time

Wait, let me reconsider. The pedestrian moves continuously from (0,0) to (0,w). If they take time `T`, they reach height `y` at time `t = y/u`.

At that moment, the bus has moved left by `v * (y/u)`.

We need to check if the point (0, y) is strictly inside the polygon that was originally at position (x, y) with x decreased by `v * (y/u)`.

Actually, let me think more clearly:

At time `t` when pedestrian reaches height `y`, their position is (0, y).
The bus has moved left by `v * t = v * (y/u)`.
So we need to check if (0, y) is strictly inside the original polygon shifted left by `v * (y/u)`.

For each height y in [0, w], we need to check if (0, y) is strictly inside the polygon with all x-coordinates decreased by `v * (y/u)`.

The pedestrian is safe if for all y in [0, w], the point (0, y) is not strictly inside the shifted polygon.

However, since the pedestrian moves continuously, we can find the minimum time by checking when they enter the "danger zone".

## Binary Search Solution

We'll binary search on the time T. For a given time T, we check if the pedestrian can cross safely.

For time T, the pedestrian reaches height y at time t = y/u (if y ≤ u*T).

At that time, the bus has moved left by v * (y/u).

We need to check if for all y in [0, min(w, u*T)], the point (0, y) is not strictly inside the polygon shifted by -v*(y/u).

Actually, the pedestrian can move at different speeds, so they can delay reaching certain heights. The optimal strategy is to reach each height as soon as possible, unless it puts them in danger.

Let me implement a solution that checks for each height whether the pedestrian is safe.

```python
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def point_in_polygon(px, py, polygon):
    """Check if point (px, py) is strictly inside the polygon"""
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    
    # Using ray casting algorithm
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        xi, yi = polygon[i]
        xj, yj = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on the boundary or inside
        if (yi > py) != (yj > py):
            # Point is on the edge or inside
            if (px < (xj - xi) * (py - yi) / (yj - yi) + xi):
                inside = not inside
    
    # Check if point is strictly inside
    # For strictly inside, we need to check if it's not on any edge
    # and if it's inside (ray casting gives inside/outside)
    # But in this problem, we care about strictly inside
    
    # Since we're doing ray casting, if we get a mixed result, check if strictly inside
    # Actually, we just need to know if it's strictly inside
    return inside

def point_is_strictly_inside(px, py, polygon):
    """Check if point (px, py) is strictly inside the polygon (not on boundary)"""
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        xi, yi = polygon[i]
        xj, yj = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on the edge
        if (py == yi and py == yj and px >= min(xi, xj) and px <= max(xi, xj)):
            return False  # on boundary
        
        # Ray casting - if point is on edge or vertex, it's not strictly inside
        if (yi > py) != (yj > py):
            if (px < (xj - xi) * (py - yi) / (yj - yi) + xi):
                inside = not inside
    
    # If we're not on any edge and ray casting says inside, it's strictly inside
    # But ray casting can be off if point is on edge
    # Actually, we need to be more careful
    
    # Check if point is inside using sign of cross products
    count = 0
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        xi, yi = polygon[i]
        xj, yj = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on edge
        if (yi == py and yj == py and xi <= px <= xj) or (yi == py and yj == py and xj <= px <= xi):
            return False
        
        # Cross product
        cross = (xj - xi) * (py - yi) - (yj - yi) * (px - xi)
        if cross > 0:
            count += 1
    
    # If count is odd, point is inside
    return count % 2 == 1

def polygon_shifted(polygon, shift):
    """Shift all points of polygon by shift in x-direction"""
    return [(x - shift, y) for x, y in polygon]

def is_pedestrian_safe(time, n, w, v, u, vertices):
    """Check if pedestrian can cross safely in time `time`"""
    # Pedestrian reaches height y at time t = y/u
    # At that time, bus has moved left by v * (y/u)
    # We need to check if (0, y) is strictly inside the shifted polygon
    
    # Maximum height pedestrian can reach in time `time`
    max_height = min(w, u * time)
    
    if max_height <= 0:
        return True
    
    # Check each height y from 0 to max_height
    for y in range(1, int(max_height) + 1):
        # Time to reach height y
        t_reach = y / u
        
        # If pedestrian hasn't reached this height yet, skip
        if t_reach > time:
            continue
            
        # Bus position at time t_reach
        bus_shift = v * t_reach
        
        # Shifted polygon
        shifted_polygon = polygon_shifted(vertices, bus_shift)
        
        # Check if (0, y) is strictly inside the shifted polygon
        if point_is_strictly_inside(0, y, shifted_polygon):
            return False
    
    return True

def solve():
    n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())
    vertices = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        vertices.append((x, y))
    
    # Binary search on time
    left, right = 0.0, w / u * 2  # Upper bound: cross without waiting
    
    # We need to find minimum time
    for _ in range(100):  # 100 iterations for precision
        mid = (left + right) / 2
        if is_pedestrian_safe(mid, n, w, v, u, vertices):
            right = mid
        else:
            left = mid
    
    print(f"{left:.10f}")

solve()
```

```python
def point_is_strictly_inside(px, py, polygon):
    """Check if point (px, py) is strictly inside the polygon"""
```

I see the issue - I'm iterating over integer heights which might miss continuous values. Let me fix this with a more accurate approach:

```python
def point_is_strictly_inside(px, py, polygon):
    """Check if point (px, py) is strictly inside the polygon using ray casting"""
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        xi, yi = polygon[i]
        xj, yj = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on edge (boundary)
        if (yi == py and yj == py and xi <= px <= xj) or (yi == py and yj == py and xj <= px <= xi):
            return False
            
        # Ray casting: if point is below one edge and above the other
        if (yi > py) != (yj > py):
            # Intersect vertical ray from point to right
            if px < (xj - xi) * (py - yi) / (yj - yi) + xi:
                inside = not inside
    
    return inside

def solve():
    n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())
    vertices = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        vertices.append((x, y))
    
    # Binary search on time
    left, right = 0.0, w / u * 2
    
    # We need to find minimum time
    for _ in range(100):
        mid = (left + right) / 2
        safe = True
        
        # Check for each height from 0 to min(w, u*mid)
        max_y = min(w, u * mid)
        
        # Check at each height y (discretized to avoid precision issues)
        y_step = 1e-6
        y = 0.0
        while y <= max_y:
            y += y_step
            t_reach = y / u
            if t_reach > mid:
                break
                
            # Bus shifted by v * t_reach
            shift = v * t_reach
            shifted_polygon = [(x - shift, y) for x, y in vertices]
            
            # Check if (0, y) is strictly inside shifted polygon
            if point_is_strictly_inside(0, y, shifted_polygon):
                safe = False
                break
        
        if safe:
            right = mid
        else:
            left = mid
    
    print(f"{left:.10f}")

solve()
```

def point_is_strictly_inside(px, py, polygon):
    """Check if point (px, py) is strictly inside the polygon using ray casting"""
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        xi, yi = polygon[i]
        xj, yj = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on edge (boundary)
        if (yi == py and yj == py and xi <= px <= xj) or (yi == py and yj == py and xj <= px <= xi):
            return False
            
        # Ray casting: if point is below one edge and above the other
        if (yi > py) != (yj > py):
            # Intersect vertical ray from point to right
            if px < (xj - xi) * (py - yi) / (yj - yi) + xi:
                inside = not inside
    
    return inside

def solve():
    n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())
    vertices = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        vertices.append((x, y))
    
    # Binary search on time
    left, right = 0.0, w / u * 2
    
    # We need to find minimum time
    for _ in range(100):
        mid = (left + right) / 2
        safe = True
        
        # Check for each height from 0 to min(w, u*mid)
        max_y = min(w, u * mid)
        
        # Check at each height y (discretized to avoid precision issues)
        y_step = 1e-6
        y = 0.0
        while y <= max_y:
            y += y_step
            t_reach = y / u
            if t_reach > mid:
                break
                
            # Bus shifted by v * t_reach
            shift = v * t_reach
            shifted_polygon = [(x - shift, y) for x, y in vertices]

            if point_is_strictly_inside(0, y, shifted_polygon):
                safe = False
                break
        
        if safe:
            right = mid
        else:
            left = mid
    
    print(f"{left:.10f}")

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a bus moving leftward (decreasing x) with speed v
2. A pedestrian starts at (0,0) and needs to reach (0,w) by moving vertically
3. The pedestrian can move with speed at most u
4. We need to find the minimum time for the pedestrian to cross safely (not hit by the bus)

## Key Insights

1. The pedestrian is hit if their position lies strictly inside the bus polygon
2. Since the bus moves leftward with speed v, at time t, the bus's x-coordinates decrease by vt
3. The pedestrian can adjust their speed at any time to avoid the bus
4. The critical insight is that we need to find when the pedestrian can safely cross - this happens when the bus doesn't reach the pedestrian's position

## Approach

The pedestrian needs to cross from (0,0) to (0,w). The time needed is at least w/u (minimum time if moving at maximum speed).

The bus will "threaten" the pedestrian at different times. For each possible crossing time, we need to check if the pedestrian can safely cross.

Let's think about when the pedestrian is at position (0, y) at time t:
- The pedestrian's position at time t is (0, y) where y = u * t' for some t' ≤ t
- The bus at time t has its x-coordinates decreased by v*t

For the pedestrian to be safe at time t, their position (0, y) should not lie strictly inside the bus polygon at time t.

The key insight is that we need to find the earliest time when the pedestrian can reach (0,w) without being hit. This happens when the bus moves far enough left that it doesn't reach the pedestrian's position.

## Algorithm

1. For each possible vertical position y (from 0 to w), we need to check if the bus can reach that position at some time t
2. At time t, the bus has x-coordinate decreased by v*t
3. For the pedestrian at position (0,y), the bus at time t will pass through that point only if the bus's horizontal position at that time can reach x=0 with y-coordinate y
4. We need to find the minimum time t such that for all positions the pedestrian occupies, they are not strictly inside the bus

Actually, let me reconsider. The pedestrian moves vertically. At time t, they are at (0, y) where y = u * t' for some t' ≤ t.

The bus at time t has all x-coordinates decreased by v*t. So the bus's position at time t is the original polygon with all x-coordinates shifted left by v*t.

The pedestrian is safe at time t if at no point during their journey do they lie strictly inside the bus polygon.

A simpler approach: the pedestrian needs to reach (0,w) in time T, so they spend w/u time if they move at maximum speed. But if the bus is too close, they might need to wait.

Actually, the pedestrian can wait at the start or middle of their journey. The optimal strategy is to move at speed u and find the minimum time T such that for all times t' in [0,T], the point (0, y(t')) is not strictly inside the bus at time (T - t').

Wait, let me think again.

The bus moves left at speed v. At time t, it has x-coordinates reduced by v*t.

The pedestrian starts at (0,0) at time 0 and reaches (0,w) at time T.

At time τ (where 0 ≤ τ ≤ T), the pedestrian is at position (0, y) where y = u * τ (if moving at constant speed).

The bus at time τ has x-coordinates reduced by v*τ.

So we need to check if the point (0,y) lies strictly inside the bus polygon after shifting x-coordinates by -v*τ.

The pedestrian is safe if for all τ in [0,T], the point (0,y) where y = u * τ at time τ, the point does not lie strictly inside the bus after shifting.

For each possible time T, we can check if there's any τ in [0,T] such that (0, u*τ) lies strictly inside the shifted polygon at time τ.

We need to find the minimum T such that no such τ exists.

This is a binary search problem on T.

For a given T, for each τ in [0,T], check if (0, u*τ) lies strictly inside the bus polygon after shifting all x-coordinates by -v*τ.

But we don't need to check all τ, we just need to check critical points - where the pedestrian might be hit.

The critical times occur when the pedestrian is at positions where the bus's edge passes through (0,y).

## Implementation

1. Binary search on the answer T
2. For each candidate T, check if any point (0, u*τ) for τ in [0,T] lies strictly inside the shifted bus polygon
3. The shifting is: for each vertex (x,y), it becomes (x - v*τ, y)

```python
def point_in_polygon(point, polygon):
    """Check if point is strictly inside polygon (not on boundary)"""
    x, y = point
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        xi, yi = polygon[i]
        xj, yj = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on the line segment
        # Point is on edge if it lies on the line between (xi,yi) and (xj,yj)
        # and between the endpoints
        cross_product = (xj - xi) * (y - yi) - (yj - yi) * (x - xi)
        
        # If cross product is 0, point is on the line
        if abs(cross_product) < 1e-9:
            # Check if point is between the endpoints
            dot_product = (x - xi) * (xj - xi) + (y - yi) * (yj - yi)
            if 0 <= dot_product <= (xj - xi) ** 2 + (yj - yi) ** 2:
                return False  # On boundary, not strictly inside
        
        # Check orientation (cross product sign)
        if cross_product > 0:
            inside = not inside
    
    return inside

def point_in_polygon_strict(point, polygon):
    """Check if point is strictly inside polygon (not on boundary)"""
    x, y = point
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        xi, yi = polygon[i]
        xj, yj = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on the line segment
        cross_product = (xj - xi) * (y - yi) - (yj - yi) * (x - xi)
        
        if abs(cross_product) < 1e-9:
            # Check if point is between the endpoints
            dot_product = (x - xi) * (xj - xi) + (y - yi) * (yj - yi)
            if 0 <= dot_product <= (xj - xi) ** 2 + (yj - yi) ** 2:
                return False  # On boundary
        
        # Check orientation
        if cross_product > 0:
            inside = not inside
    
    return inside

def is_safe(t, polygon, v, u, w):
    """Check if pedestrian can safely cross in time t"""
    # At time t, the pedestrian reaches (0, w)
    # But the threat comes at different times τ in [0,t]
    # At time τ, pedestrian is at (0, u*τ)
    # The bus at time τ has x-coordinates reduced by v*τ
    
    # We need to check if at any τ in [0,t], (0, u*τ) lies strictly inside the shifted polygon
    # The shifted polygon has vertices (x - v*τ, y)
    
    # We only need to check critical times - when the pedestrian is at certain y-coordinates
    # where the bus edge passes through x=0
    
    # Check at each possible y position that the pedestrian might be hit on
    # The pedestrian moves at speed u, so at time τ, they're at y = u*τ
    
    # We can also check endpoints and when the bus passes through x=0
    # But let's just check a few key points
    
    # Check at y = 0 (start)
    if u * 0 >= 0 and u * 0 <= w:
        # Check if (0,0) is strictly inside the bus at time 0
        # Shift by v*0 = 0
        shifted_polygon = [(x, y) for x, y in polygon]
        if point_in_polygon_strict((0, 0), shifted_polygon):
            return False
    
    # Check at y = w (end)
    if u * t >= 0 and u * t <= w:
        # Check if (0,w) is strictly inside the bus at time t
        # Shift by v*t
        shifted_polygon = [(x - v * t, y) for x, y in polygon]
        if point_in_polygon_strict((0, w), shifted_polygon):
            return False
    
    # Check if there's any time τ where pedestrian is strictly inside
    # We need to find if there's a τ in (0,t) such that (0, u*τ) is strictly inside the shifted polygon
    
    # Since this is continuous, we can use binary search or check key points
    # But for efficiency, let's just check a few critical points
    
    # Actually, we can check when the bus passes through x=0
    # For each edge, find when it passes through x=0
    # This might be complex
    
    # Simpler approach: check if at any time τ, the point is strictly inside
    # We can check the endpoints and the times when the bus edge passes through y=0
    
    # Check if storm passes through y=0 at any point
    # Actually, let's just check a uniform grid of points to be safe
    
    # Check at τ = t/2
    if u * (t / 2) >= 0 and u * (t / 2) <= w:
        shifted_polygon = [(x - v * (t / 2), y) for x, y in polygon]
        if point_in_polygon_strict((0, u * (t / 2)), shifted_polygon):
            return False
    
    # Check if safe for the entire journey
    for τ in [t * 0.1, t * 0.2, t * 0.3, t * 0.4, t * 0.5, t * 0.6, t * 0.7, t * 0.8, t * 0.9]:
        if 0 <= τ <= t:
            y = u * τ
            if y < 0 or y > w:
                continue
            shifted_polygon = [(x - v * τ, y) for x, y in polygon]
            if point_in_polygon_strict((0, y), shifted_polygon):
                return False
    
    return True

def solve():
    n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())
    polygon = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        polygon.append((x, y))
    
    # The minimum time is at least w/u (if no threat)
    # But if the bus comes too close, we might need more time
    
    # The maximum time we need to consider is when the pedestrian waits at the start
    # If the bus is very close, we might need to wait until it has moved far enough
    
    # Binary search on the answer
    left = 0.0
    right = w / u + 10000  # Safe upper bound
    
    # We need high precision
    for _ in range(100):  # 100 iterations for good precision
        mid = (left + right) / 2
        if is_safe(mid, polygon, v, u, w):
            right = mid
        else:
            left = mid
    
    print(f"{left:.10f}")
```

For a given time T, we need to check if the pedestrian can cross safely in time T.

The pedestrian moves from (0,0) to (0,w) in time T. At time τ, they're at (0, u*τ), and the bus at that time is shifted with x-coordinates decreased by v*τ.

```python
def point_in_polygon_strict(point, polygon):
    """Check if point is strictly inside polygon (not on boundary)"""
    x, y = point
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        xi, yi = polygon[i]
        xj, yj = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on the edge
        cross_product = (xj - xi) * (y - yi) - (yj - yi) * (x - xi)
        
        # If cross product is very close to 0, check if on segment
        if abs(cross_product) < 1e-9:
            # Check if point is between endpoints
            dot_product = (x - xi) * (xj - xi) + (y - yi) * (yj - yi)
            if dot_product >= 0 and dot_product <= (xj - xi)**2 + (yj - yi)**2:
                return False  # On boundary
        
        # Check orientation
        if cross_product > 0:
            inside = not inside
    
    return inside

def is_safe(t, polygon, v, u, w):
    """Check if pedestrian can safely cross in time t"""
    # Check at key points during journey
    # We'll check at specific times to avoid computational issues
    
    # Check at position (0, y) where y = u * τ for different τ
    # We need to check if any such point lies strictly inside the shifted polygon
    
    # Check at start (τ=0)
    if point_in_polygon_strict((0, 0), [(x, y) for x, y in polygon]):
        return False
    
    # Check at end (τ=t)
    if t > 0:  # Only if we have time to reach end
        end_y = u * t
        if end_y > w:
            end_y = w
        # But pedestrian reaches (0,w) at time t
        # At time t, the bus has shifted by v*t
        shifted_polygon = [(x - v * t, y) for x, y in polygon]
        if point_in_polygon_strict((0, w), shifted_polygon):
            return False
    
    # Check at midpoint
    if t > 0:
        mid_y = u * (t / 2)
        if mid_y > w:
            mid_y = w
        shifted_polygon = [(x - v * (t / 2), y) for x, y in polygon]
        if point_in_polygon_strict((0, mid_y), shifted_polygon):
            return False
    
    # Check at times when edges might pass through (0, y)
    # But for practical purposes, we can check a few points
    for i in range(1, 10):
        τ = t * i / 10
        if τ > t:
            continue
        y = u * τ
        if y < 0 or y > w:
            continue
        shifted_polygon = [(x - v * τ, y) for x, y in polygon]
        if point_in_polygon_strict((0, y), shifted_polygon):
            return False
    
    return True

n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())
polygon = []
for _ in range(n):

    polygon.append((x, y))
```

def point_in_polygon_strict(point, polygon):
    """Check if point is strictly inside polygon (not on boundary)"""
    x, y = point
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        xi, yi = polygon[i]
        xj, yj = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on the edge
        cross_product = (xj - xi) * (y - yi) - (yj - yi) * (x - xi)
        
        # If cross product is very close to 0, check if on segment
        if abs(cross_product) < 1e-9:
            # Check if point is between endpoints
            dot_product = (x - xi) * (xj - xi) + (y - yi) * (yj - yi)
            if dot_product >= 0 and dot_product <= (xj - xi)**2 + (yj - yi)**2:
                return False  # On boundary
        
        # Check orientation
        if cross_product > 0:
            inside = not inside
    
    return inside

def is_safe(t, polygon, v, u, w):
    """Check if pedestrian can safely cross in time t"""
    # Check at key points during journey
    # We'll check at specific times to avoid computational issues
    
    # Check at position (0, y) where y = u * τ for different τ
    # We need to check if any such point lies strictly inside the shifted polygon
    
    # Check at start (τ=0)
    if point_in_polygon_strict((0, 0), [(x, y) for x, y in polygon]):
        return False
    
    # Check at end (τ=t)
    if t > 0:  # Only if we have time to reach end
        end_y = u * t
        if end_y > w:
            end_y = w
        # But pedestrian reaches (0,w) at time t
        # At time t, the bus has shifted by v*t
        shifted_polygon = [(x - v * t, y) for x, y in polygon]
        if point_in_polygon_strict((0, w), shifted_polygon):
            return False
    
    # Check at midpoint
    if t > 0:
        mid_y = u * (t / 2)
        if mid_y > w:
            mid_y = w
        shifted_polygon = [(x - v * (t / 2), y) for x, y in polygon]
        if point_in_polygon_strict((0, mid_y), shifted_polygon):
            return False
    
    # Check at times when edges might pass through (0, y)
    # But for practical purposes, we can check a few points
    for i in range(1, 10):
        τ = t * i / 10
        if τ > t:
            continue
        y = u * τ
        if y < 0 or y > w:
            continue
        shifted_polygon = [(x - v * τ, y) for x, y in polygon]
        if point_in_polygon_strict((0, y), shifted_polygon):
            return False
    
    return True

And while Mishka is enjoying her trip...

Chris is a little brown bear. No one knows, where and when he met Mishka, but for a long time they are together (excluding her current trip). However, best friends are important too. John is Chris' best friend.

Once walking with his friend, John gave Chris the following problem:

At the infinite horizontal road of width w, bounded by lines y = 0 and y = w, there is a bus moving, presented as a convex polygon of n vertices. The bus moves continuously with a constant speed of v in a straight Ox line in direction of decreasing x coordinates, thus in time only x coordinates of its points are changing. Formally, after time t each of x coordinates of its points will be decreased by vt.

There is a pedestrian in the point (0, 0), who can move only by a vertical pedestrian crossing, presented as a segment connecting points (0, 0) and (0, w) with any speed not exceeding u. Thus the pedestrian can move only in a straight line Oy in any direction with any speed not exceeding u and not leaving the road borders. The pedestrian can instantly change his speed, thus, for example, he can stop instantly.

Please look at the sample note picture for better understanding.

We consider the pedestrian is hit by the bus, if at any moment the point he is located in lies strictly inside the bus polygon (this means that if the point lies on the polygon vertex or on its edge, the pedestrian is not hit by the bus).

You are given the bus position at the moment 0. Please help Chris determine minimum amount of time the pedestrian needs to cross the road and reach the point (0, w) and not to be hit by the bus.

Input

The first line of the input contains four integers n, w, v, u (3 ≤ n ≤ 10 000, 1 ≤ w ≤ 109, 1 ≤ v, u ≤ 1000) — the number of the bus polygon vertices, road width, bus speed and pedestrian speed respectively.

The next n lines describes polygon vertices in counter-clockwise order. i-th of them contains pair of integers xi and yi ( - 109 ≤ xi ≤ 109, 0 ≤ yi ≤ w) — coordinates of i-th polygon point. It is guaranteed that the polygon is non-degenerate.

Output

Print the single real t — the time the pedestrian needs to croos the road and not to be hit by the bus. The answer is considered correct if its relative or absolute error doesn't exceed 10 - 6.

Example

Input

1 2
3 1
4 3
3 4
1 4


Output

5.0000000000

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a bus moving leftward (decreasing x coordinates) with speed `v`
2. A pedestrian starts at (0,0) and needs to reach (0,w) by moving vertically
3. The pedestrian can move with speed at most `u`
4. We need to find the minimum time for pedestrian to cross without being hit by the bus
5. Being hit means being strictly inside the bus polygon (not on edges/vertices)

## Key Insights

1. The bus moves in the negative x-direction at speed `v`
2. At time `t`, the entire bus shifts left by `v*t`
3. The pedestrian moves vertically from (0,0) to (0,w) at speed `u`
4. The pedestrian will be hit if at any moment during their journey, they are strictly inside the bus polygon after the bus has moved

## Approach

The pedestrian crosses in time T = w/u (minimum possible time if no constraints).

However, at time T, the bus has moved left by v*T. The pedestrian's path is from (0,0) to (0,w).

For each position (0,y) along the pedestrian's path, we need to check if at the moment when the pedestrian reaches (0,y), the point (0,y) is strictly inside the bus polygon.

The key insight is that if at any point on the pedestrian's path the point is in the bus at the appropriate time, the pedestrian gets hit.

For a point (0,y) to be safely passed, there must be a time when the pedestrian is at (0,y) and the bus has moved such that (0,y) is outside the bus.

Let's think about when the pedestrian reaches height y: time = y/u.

At that time, the bus has moved left by v*(y/u) = v*y/u.

So we need to check if at x = -v*y/u, the point (0,y) is strictly outside the bus at that horizontal position.

Actually, we need to check if (0,y) is inside the bus polygon when the bus is at x = -v*(y/u).

But since the pedestrian moves continuously, we need to ensure no collision occurs during the entire journey.

- At that time, the bus has moved left by v * t_y = v * y / u
- So we check if (0,y) is strictly inside the polygon when translated by -v * y / u in the x-direction

If for no y is (0,y) strictly inside, then T is safe.

We can use binary search on T to find the minimum safe crossing time.

```python
import sys

def point_in_polygon(x, y, poly):
    """Check if point (x,y) is strictly inside the polygon"""
    n = len(poly)
    inside = False
    
    # Use ray casting algorithm
    p1x, p1y = poly[0]
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        p2x, p2y = poly[i % n]
        # Check if point is on the edge or vertex
        if (y > min(p1y, p2y)) and (y <= max(p1y, p2y)) and (x <= max(p1x, p2x)) and (y != min(p1y, p2y) or x <= max(p1x, p2x)):
            if y == min(p1y, p2y):
                if y == max(p1y, p2y):
                    # On the edge
                    if x >= min(p1x, p2x) and x <= max(p1x, p2x):
                        return False  # On edge or vertex
            else:
                # Calculate intersection point
                if p1y != p2y:
                    xinters = (y - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
                    if x == xinters:
                        return False  # On edge or vertex
                else:
                    # Horizontal line
                    if y == p1y:
                        if x >= min(p1x, p2x) and x <= max(p1x, p2x):
                            return False  # On edge or vertex
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    
    return inside

def is_strictly_inside(x, y, poly):
    """Check if point (x,y) is strictly inside the polygon (not on boundary)"""
    n = len(poly)
    inside = False
    
    p1x, p1y = poly[0]
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        p2x, p2y = poly[i % n]
        
        # Check if point is strictly inside
        # Simple check: point is strictly inside if all edges are on the same side
        # But we need to handle edge cases
        
        # Simple ray casting - if ray crosses an edge, count it
        if (p1y > y) != (p2y > y):
            if p1y <= y <= p2y or p2y <= y <= p1y:
                x_intersect = (y - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
                if x < x_intersect:
                    inside = not inside
        
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    
    # If inside is True, the point might be on boundary
    # We need to check if it's strictly inside
    
    # Simple: if ray casting gives True, check if point is on any edge
    # But this is getting complex
    
    # Alternative: check if point is strictly inside by looking at edge crossings
    # and ensuring no edge passes through the point
    
    crossings = 0
    p1x, p1y = poly[0]
    for i in range(1, n):
        p2x, p2y = poly[i]
        
        # Check if ray from (x,y) to right crosses edge
        if (p1y > y) != (p2y > y):
            x_intersect = (y - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
            if x < x_intersect:
                crossings += 1
        
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    
    # Point is strictly inside if odd number of crossings and not on boundary
    # We need to check if point is on any edge
    
    # Check if point is on any edge
    for i in range(n):
        p1x, p1y = poly[i]
        p2x, p2y = poly[(i+1) % n]
        
        # Check if (x,y) lies on the segment
        if p1x == p2x:  # Vertical edge
            if x == p1x and min(p1y, p2y) <= y <= max(p1y, p2y):
                return False
        elif p1y == p2y:  # Horizontal edge
            if y == p1y and min(p1x, p2x) <= x <= max(p1x, p2x):
                return False
        else:  # Diagonal edge
            # Check if point lies on line
            if ((y - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) == (x - p1x) * (p2y - p1y)) and \
               (min(p1x, p2x) <= x <= max(p1x, p2x)) and \
               (min(p1y, p2y) <= y <= max(p1y, p2y)):
                return False
    
    return crossings % 2 == 1

def point_on_segment(px, py, x, y):
    """Check if point (x,y) lies on segment from (px,py) to (px+dx,py+dy)"""
    dx = x - px
    dy = y - py
    
    # Check if point is collinear and within bounds
    if dx == 0 and dy == 0:
        return True
    
    # Check collinearity
    if abs(dx * (py - y) - dy * (px - x)) > 1e-9:
        return False
    
    # Check if point is within bounds
    p2x, p2y = px + dx, py + dy
    minx, maxx = min(px, p2x), max(px, p2x)
    miny, maxy = min(py, p2y), max(py, p2y)
    
    return minx <= x <= maxx and miny <= y <= maxy

def is_inside_or_on_boundary(x, y, poly):
    """Check if point is on boundary or inside"""
    n = len(poly)
    
    # Check if on any edge
    for i in range(n):
        px, py = poly[i]
        px2, py2 = poly[(i+1) % n]
        
        if point_on_segment(px, py, x, y):
            return True
    
    # Check if strictly inside using ray casting
    crossings = 0
    p1x, p1y = poly[0]
    for i in range(1, n):
        p2x, p2y = poly[i]
        
        if (p1y > y) != (p2y > y):
            x_intersect = (y - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
            if x < x_intersect:
                crossings += 1
        
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    
    return crossings % 2 == 1

def is_strictly_inside(x, y, poly):
    """Check if point is strictly inside (not on boundary)"""
    if is_inside_or_on_boundary(x, y, poly):
        return False
    return True

def solve():
    # Read input
    n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())
    poly = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        poly.append((x, y))
    
    # If pedestrian crosses immediately (time = w/u), check if safe
    # We need to check if for each y in [0,w], point (0,y) is strictly inside
    # when bus is at x = -v*y/u
    
    # Binary search on time T
    left, right = 0.0, 10**12
    
    # Safety check: if pedestrian can cross in w/u time
    min_time = w / u
    
    # Check if crossing in min_time is safe
    safe = True
    for y in [0, w/2, w]:  # Check key points
        if y == 0 or y == w:
            # At endpoints, should not be hit
            continue
        t = y / u
        bus_x = -v * t
        if is_strictly_inside(bus_x, y, poly):
            safe = False
            break
    
    if safe:
        print(f"{min_time:.10f}")
        return
    
    # For safety, we need to find when the bus moves far enough
    # such that the pedestrian's path is not strictly inside
    # We can use binary search to find minimum T
    
    # Actually, we need to check all points y in (0,w)
    # Since there are infinitely many, we can sample key points
    # where collision might occur - typically when the pedestrian crosses
    # at positions where the bus edge passes through the path
    
    # Binary search on T
    for _ in range(100):  # 100 iterations for precision
        mid = (left + right) / 2
        
        # Check if time mid is safe
        safe = True
        for y in [w/4, w/2, 3*w/4]:
            if y == 0 or y == w:
                continue
            t = y / u
            # When pedestrian reaches height y, time is t
            # Bus has moved left by v * t
            bus_x = -v * t
            if is_strictly_inside(bus_x, y, poly):
                safe = False
                break
        
        if safe:
            right = mid
        else:
            left = mid
    
    print(f"{(left + right) / 2:.10f}")

solve()
```

Wait, I'm making this too complicated. Let me reconsider with a simpler approach.

The key insight is that for the pedestrian to be safe, at each height y, when they reach (0,y) at time y/u, the point (0,y) should not be strictly inside the bus polygon when the bus has moved left by v*(y/u).

We need to find the minimum time T such that for all y in [0,w], the point (0,y) is not strictly inside the polygon translated left by v*y/u.

Since the pedestrian moves continuously, if the crossing time is too short, they'll be hit. We can binary search on T.

```python
import sys

def point_on_segment(px, py, x, y):
    """Check if point (x,y) lies on segment from (px,py) to (px2,py2)"""
    px2, py2 = px, py
    if px == px2 and py == py2:
        return x == px and y == py
    
    # Check collinearity
    # Cross product should be zero
    cross = (x - px) * (py2 - py) - (y - py) * (px2 - px)
    if abs(cross) > 1e-9:
        return False
    
    # Check if point is within bounds
    min_x, max_x = min(px, px2), max(px, px2)
    min_y, max_y = min(py, py2), max(py, py2)
    
    return min_x <= x <= max_x and min_y <= y <= max_y

def is_strictly_inside(x, y, poly):
    """Check if point (x,y) is strictly inside the polygon (not on boundary)"""
    n = len(poly)
    
    # Check if on any edge
    for i in range(n):
        px, py = poly[i]
        px2, py2 = poly[(i+1) % n]
        
        if point_on_segment(px, py, x, y):
            return False
    
    # Use ray casting to check if strictly inside
    crossings = 0
    p1x, p1y = poly[0]
    for i in range(1, n):
        p2x, p2y = poly[i]
        
        # Check if ray from (x,y) to right crosses edge
        if (p1y > y) != (p2y > y):
            # Calculate intersection point
            x_intersect = (y - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
            if x < x_intersect:
                crossings += 1
        
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    
    return crossings % 2 == 1

def solve():
    n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())
    poly = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        poly.append((x, y))
    
    # Binary search on crossing time
    left, right = 0.0, 10**12
    
    # We'll do binary search for minimum safe time
    for _ in range(100):  # high precision
        mid = (left + right) / 2
        
        # Check if crossing in time mid is safe
        safe = True
        # Check key points in the path
        # We'll check at three key positions: w/4, w/2, 3w/4
        for y in [w/4, w/2, 3*w/4]:
            if y == 0 or y == w:
                continue
            # Time to reach height y
            t = y / u
            # Bus has moved left by v*t
            bus_x = -v * t  # x position when the bus has moved left by v*t
            
            # Check if (bus_x, y) is strictly inside the polygon
            if is_strictly_inside(bus_x, y, poly):
                safe = False
                break
        
        if safe:
            right = mid
        else:
            left = mid
    
    print(f"{(left + right) / 2:.10f}")

solve()
```

import sys

def point_on_segment(px, py, x, y):
    """Check if point (x,y) lies on segment from (px,py) to (px2,py2)"""
    px2, py2 = px, py
    if px == px2 and py == py2:
        return x == px and y == py
    
    # Check collinearity
    # Cross product should be zero
    cross = (x - px) * (py2 - py) - (y - py) * (px2 - px)
    if abs(cross) > 1e-9:
        return False
    
    # Check if point is within bounds
    min_x, max_x = min(px, px2), max(px, px2)
    min_y, max_y = min(py, py2), max(py, py2)
    
    return min_x <= x <= max_x and min_y <= y <= max_y

def is_strictly_inside(x, y, poly):
    """Check if point (x,y) is strictly inside the polygon (not on boundary)"""
    n = len(poly)
    
    # Check if on any edge
    for i in range(n):
        px, py = poly[i]
        px2, py2 = poly[(i+1) % n]
        
        if point_on_segment(px, py, x, y):
            return False
    
    # Use ray casting to check if strictly inside
    crossings = 0
    p1x, p1y = poly[0]
    for i in range(1, n):
        p2x, p2y = poly[i]
        
        # Check if ray from (x,y) to right crosses edge
        if (p1y > y) != (p2y > y):
            # Calculate intersection point
            x_intersect = (y - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
            if x < x_intersect:
                crossings += 1
        
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    
    return crossings % 2 == 1

def solve():
    n, w, v, u = map(int, input().split())
    poly = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        poly.append((x, y))
    
    # Binary search on crossing time
    left, right = 0.0, 10**12
    
    # We'll do binary search for minimum safe time
    for _ in range(100):  # high precision
        mid = (left + right) / 2
        
        # Check if crossing in time mid is safe
        safe = True
        # Check key points in the path
        # We'll check at three key positions: w/4, w/2, 3w/4
        for y in [w/4, w/2, 3*w/4]:
            if y == 0 or y == w:
                continue
            # Time to reach height y
            t = y / u
            # Bus has moved left by v*t
            bus_x = -v * t  # x position when the bus has moved left by v*t
            
            # Check if (bus_x, y) is strictly inside the polygon
            if is_strictly_inside(bus_x, y, poly):
                safe = False
                break
        
        if safe:
            right = mid
        else:
            left = mid
    
    print(f"{(left + right) / 2:.10f}")

Once walking with his friend, John gave Chris the following problem:

At the infinite horizontal road of width w, bounded by lines y = 0 and y = w, there is a bus moving, presented as a convex polygon of n vertices. The bus moves continuously with a constant speed of v in a straight Ox line in direction of decreasing x coordinates, thus in time only x coordinates of its points are changing. Formally, after time t each of x coordinates of its points will be decreased by vt.

There is a pedestrian in the point (0, 0), who can move only by a vertical pedestrian crossing, presented as a segment connecting points (0, 0) and (0, w) with any speed not exceeding u. Thus the pedestrian can move only in a straight line Oy in any direction with any speed not exceeding u and not leaving the road borders. The pedestrian can instantly change his speed, thus, for example, he can stop instantly.

Please look at the sample note picture for better understanding.

We consider the pedestrian is hit by the bus, if at any moment the point he is located in lies strictly inside the bus polygon (this means that if the point lies on the polygon vertex or on its edge, the pedestrian is not hit by the bus).

You are given the bus position at the moment 0. Please help Chris determine minimum amount of time the pedestrian needs to cross the road and reach the point (0, w) and not to be hit by the bus.

Input

The first line of the input contains four integers n, w, v, u (3 ≤ n ≤ 10 000, 1 ≤ w ≤ 109, 1 ≤ v, u ≤ 1000) — the number of the bus polygon vertices, road width, bus speed and pedestrian speed respectively.

The next n lines describes polygon vertices in counter-clockwise order. i-th of them contains pair of integers xi and yi ( - 109 ≤ xi ≤ 109, 0 ≤ yi ≤ w) — coordinates of i-th polygon point. It is guaranteed that the polygon is non-degenerate.

Output

Print the single real t — the time the pedestrian needs to croos the road and not to be hit by the bus. The answer is considered correct if its relative or absolute error doesn't exceed 10 - 6.

Example

Input

1 2
3 1

3 4
1 4

5.0000000000

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a bus moving horizontally (decreasing x-direction) with speed `v`
2. A pedestrian starts at (0,0) and needs to reach (0,w) moving vertically
3. The pedestrian can move with speed at most `u`
4. We need to find the minimum time for the pedestrian to cross safely (not hit by the bus)
5. A hit occurs only if the pedestrian's position is strictly inside the bus polygon

## Key Insights

1. Since the pedestrian moves vertically and the bus moves horizontally, we need to find when the bus's horizontal position will "block" the pedestrian's path.

2. The pedestrian crosses in time `t = w/u` if they move at maximum speed, but they might need to wait or move faster to avoid the bus.

3. Actually, the pedestrian can move at any speed ≤ u, so the minimum time to cross is `w/u`, but they need to avoid being hit.

4. The key insight: at any time `t`, the bus has moved left by `v*t`. So the bus polygon is shifted to the left by `v*t`.

5. The pedestrian is hit if at any moment `0 ≤ τ ≤ t`, their position `(0, y(τ))` is strictly inside the shifted bus polygon.

6. Since the pedestrian moves from (0,0) to (0,w), we can use binary search on the time `t`.

7. For a given time `t`, we need to check if there exists a point (0, y) where `0 < y < w` that is strictly inside the bus polygon at time `t`.

8. The pedestrian is safe if for every point (0, y) on their path, either:
   - The point is not inside the shifted bus polygon, or
   - The pedestrian reaches that point before the bus "covers" it

## Algorithm

1. For each time `t`, the bus is at position `(-v*t, y)`, so we need to check if any point `(0, y)` with `0 < y < w` is strictly inside the shifted polygon.

2. A point `(0, y)` is strictly inside if it's inside the polygon after shifting all vertices by `(-v*t, 0)`.

3. We can check if a point is strictly inside by using cross products to determine if it's on the "positive" side of all edges.

4. However, there's a simpler insight: the pedestrian is safe if at time `t`, the entire vertical segment (0,0) to (0,w) is not strictly inside the shifted polygon.

```python
def point_in_polygon(x, y, polygon):
    """Check if point (x,y) is strictly inside the polygon (not on boundary)"""
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    p1x, p1y = polygon[0]
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        p2x, p2y = polygon[i % n]
        if y > min(p1y, p2y):
            if y <= max(p1y, p2y):
                if x <= max(p1x, p2x):
                    if p1y != p2y:
                        xints = (y - p1y) * (p2x - p1x) / (p2y - p1y) + p1x
                    if x > xints:
                        inside = not inside
        p1x, p1y = p2x, p2y
    return inside

def point_strictly_inside(x, y, polygon):
    """Check if point (x,y) is strictly inside the polygon (not on boundary)"""
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = polygon[i]
        x2, y2 = polygon[j]
        
        # Check if point is on the edge
        if y == y1 and y == y2:
            if x1 <= x <= x2 or x2 <= x <= x1:
                return False  # On edge or vertex
        
        # Check if point is on the left side of edge
        cross = (x2 - x1) * (y - y1) - (y2 - y1) * (x - x1)
        if cross == 0:
            # Point is on the line - check if it's within segment bounds
            if x >= min(x1, x2) and x <= max(x1, x2) and y >= min(y1, y2) and y <= max(y1, y2):
                return False
        elif cross > 0:
            inside = not inside
    
    return inside and inside  # This is wrong

def point_strictly_inside(x, y, polygon):
    """Check if point (x,y) is strictly inside the polygon (not on boundary)"""
    n = len(polygon)
    inside = False
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = polygon[i]
        x2, y2 = polygon[j]
        
        # Check cross product to see which side of edge we're on
        cross = (x2 - x1) * (y - y1) - (y2 - y1) * (x - x1)
        if cross == 0:  # Point is on the line
            # Check if point is in the segment
            if min(x1, x2) <= x <= max(x1, x2) and min(y1, y2) <= y <= max(y1, y2):
                return False
        elif cross > 0:
            inside = not inside
    
    return inside

def is_safe(time, n, w, v, u, vertices):
    """Check if pedestrian can cross safely in time `time`"""
    # The pedestrian crosses at speed u, so at time t, they are at position (0, (u*t)/w * w) = (0, u*t)
    # But they can go slower, so we need to find if there's any point they pass through that is strictly inside the bus.
    
    # Actually, the pedestrian chooses their path. The critical question is: is there any point (0,y) with 0 < y < w 
    # that is strictly inside the bus at any time during their journey?
    
    # At time t, the bus has moved to x = -v*t. So we shift all vertices left by v*t.
    shift_x = -v * time
    
    # Check if the pedestrian's path (vertical line x=0) intersects the shifted polygon strictly inside
    # We need to check if there's any y in (0,w) such that (0,y) is strictly inside the shifted polygon
    # This happens if the polygon at time t has a "gap" or if it crosses the line x=0
    
    # More precisely, we need to check if the vertical line x=0 intersects the shifted polygon
    # at any point strictly inside the polygon.
    
    # A simpler approach: the pedestrian is safe if the entire vertical segment from (0,0) to (0,w) 
    # is not strictly inside the bus at any time.
    
    # Actually, we can use the fact that if at time t, the bus has shifted to x=-v*t,
    # then we need to check if the line x=0 intersects the shifted polygon at any point y in (0,w).
    
    # Convert vertices to shifted positions
    shifted = [(x + shift_x, y) for x, y in vertices]
    
    # Check if the line x=0 intersects the polygon at any interior point
    # We can check if there's any edge that crosses x=0 and the intersection is strictly inside
    
    for i in range(n):
        j = (i + 1) % n
        x1, y1 = shifted[i]
        x2, y2 = shifted[j]
        
        # Check if edge crosses x=0
        if x1 * x2 < 0:  # The edge crosses the y-axis
            # Calculate intersection point
            # x = 0
            # y = y1 + (y2-y1) * (0-x1)/(x2-x1)
            if x2 - x1 == 0:
                continue  # vertical edge
            if x1 == 0 or x2 == 0:
                # edge passes through x=0
                y_intersect = y1 + (y2 - y1) * (0 - x1) / (x2 - x1)
                # Check if y_intersect is in (0,w)
                if 0 < y_intersect < w:
                    # The intersection point is strictly inside
                    # The pedestrian would be hit at this point
                    return False
    
    # If we get here, no intersection with strictly interior points
    return True
```

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

And while Mishka is enjoying her trip...

Chris is a little brown bear. No one knows, where and when he met Mishka, but for a long time they are together (excluding her current trip). However, best friends are important too. John is Chris' best friend.

Once walking with his friend, John gave Chris the following problem:

At the infinite horizontal road of width w, bounded by lines y = 0 and y = w, there is a bus moving, presented as a convex polygon of n vertices. The bus moves continuously with a constant speed of v in a straight Ox line in direction of decreasing x coordinates, thus in time only x coordinates of its points are changing. Formally, after time t each of x coordinates of its points will be decreased by vt.

There is a pedestrian in the point (0, 0), who can move only by a vertical pedestrian crossing, presented as a segment connecting points (0, 0) and (0, w) with any speed not exceeding u. Thus the pedestrian can move only in a straight line Oy in any direction with any speed not exceeding u and not leaving the road borders. The pedestrian can instantly change his speed, thus, for example, he can stop instantly.

Please look at the sample note picture for better understanding.

We consider the pedestrian is hit by the bus, if at any moment the point he is located in lies strictly inside the bus polygon (this means that if the point lies on the polygon vertex or on its edge, the pedestrian is not hit by the bus).

You are given the bus position at the moment 0. Please help Chris determine minimum amount of time the pedestrian needs to cross the road and reach the point (0, w) and not to be hit by the bus.

Input

The first line of the input contains four integers n, w, v, u (3 ≤ n ≤ 10 000, 1 ≤ w ≤ 109, 1 ≤ v, u ≤ 1000) — the number of the bus polygon vertices, road width, bus speed and pedestrian speed respectively.

The next n lines describes polygon vertices in counter-clockwise order. i-th of them contains pair of integers xi and yi ( - 109 ≤ xi ≤ 109, 0 ≤ yi ≤ w) — coordinates of i-th polygon point. It is guaranteed that the polygon is non-degenerate.

Output

Print the single real t — the time the pedestrian needs to croos the road and not to be hit by the bus. The answer is considered correct if its relative or absolute error doesn't exceed 10 - 6.

Example

Input

5 5 1 2
1 2
3 1
4 3
3 4
1 4


Output

5.0000000000
